# Engineering Backlog

This is the **single** backlog for cross-cutting or future action items that
emerge from reviews and planning. It is the successor to the old
`deferred-work.md`. Everything that used to land there lands here now.

Routing guidance:

- Use this file for non-urgent optimizations, refactors, or follow-ups that span
  multiple stories/epics, plus review deferrals that are real but intentionally
  not actioned in the PR that found them.
- Must-fix items to ship a story belong in that story's `Tasks / Subtasks`.
- Same-epic improvements may also be captured under the epic Tech Spec
  `Post-Review Follow-ups` section.

How items flow:

1. The BMGD/BMAD review skills (`gds-code-review`, `bmad-code-review`,
   `*-quick-dev`) append each `defer` finding to the **Deferral inbox** below,
   under a dated `### Deferred from:` heading.
2. Triage folds inbox entries into the table as a curated row (and removes the
   raw inbox note once captured). Pick items up when you next touch the area.

   **Standing triage cadence (per-PR, adopted 2026-07-21 per #545 / audit
   AUD-004).** A PR that parks a `defer` finding triages its OWN new inbox
   entries before it merges: fold each into a curated row (with a mirror issue
   when unresolved), mark it absorbed or superseded with a pointer to the
   destination, or retire it with a one-line factual reason. The inbox is a
   hand-off buffer for the change in flight, so it holds only entries from work
   that has not yet merged and does not accrete into a standing archive. The
   weekly-batch alternative
   was the recorded dissent (audit §14a Round 3); per-PR was adopted because the
   inbox had already accreted ~90 dated sections over two weeks under batch-only
   sweeping, the exact invisible-work rot AUD-004 describes: a batch that never
   quite runs leaves findings untracked by both backlogs. The one-time clearing
   pass that adopted this cadence is #545.
3. **GitHub mirror (standing rule, 2026-07-15):** every curated row that is not
   finished has a matching GitHub issue, recorded in the `GH` column as `#NNN`,
   for an issue in this repository. Work on the engine or the components
   lives in their own repositories' backlogs (see item 7).
   Finished means `done`, `resolved`, `shipped-v1` (its remainders live on
   their own rows), or superseded; every other status is open work and needs a
   live issue. Create the issue when the row lands, with a plain title (no
   `[Bug]:` or `[P2]` style prefix: labels carry both), a type label (`bug` for
   a defect-shaped `review-deferral` or a `bug`; `enhancement` for a
   `feature-request` or an improvement-shaped `review-deferral`, `perf` or
   `design-decision`; `task` for a `task` row or other internal work no player
   would report; `parity` for implemented behavior that diverges from 1994;
   `documentation` for docs and process work), the row's priority as a
   `P1`/`P2`/`P3` label, and a body laid out in the issue form that type label
   names (AGENTS.md, "Filing issues"), with the row's notes in its main field
   and the story id in its backlog or reference field. The row's `Priority` cell owns the P label: when a push to
   `main` changes this file, and weekly, `backlog-priority-labels.yml` sets the
   matching label on each open issue a row cites, removes any other P label and
   `needs-triage`, and fails the run when a row cites an issue that is no
   longer open (a finished row still carrying its number, or an unfinished
   row whose issue closed), so the drift gets trued up. To change a
   priority, edit the row. An issue no row cites (a community report) arrives
   with `needs-triage` from its template, and triage swaps that for a P label.
   An issue the Rust-only engine changes majorly (a TypeScript-engine rule, the
   TypeScript codecs, the crate survey, the 4.0 roadmap) belongs to the `4.x`
   milestone, and its row Notes lead with **Post-3.0** and the reason (the
   `post-3.0` label was retired on 2026-10-29 in favor of milestones); the P
   label still orders it among the 4.x work. A bug that only affects the
   TypeScript fallback also carries the `closes with TS removal` label.
   Every issue, open or closed, has a milestone: `4.x` (the current release
   line: every 4.y release, and any work already in progress),
   `Triage` (new or undecided, each gets a decision and leaves), `5.0.0` (the
   Tall lot, the wide lot, audio driven by engine events and a small web
   legibility track; the Visual Renaissance's renderer swap and art overhaul
   were cut on 2026-11-12), `6.0.0` (deeper simulation: economy, transport,
   tenants and 1994 parity), `Backlog` (deliberately parked enhancements with
   no target version), or `1.x`, `2.x` and `3.0.0` for closed work by release
   line. The milestones were renamed on 2026-11-11 (`3.x` became `4.0.0`,
   `4.x` became `5.0.0`, `5.x` became `6.0.0`), and `4.0.0` became `4.x` when
   4.0.0 shipped (2026-11-15). A closed duplicate or not-planned issue takes
   the milestone of the line it was closed in. A bug is never in `Backlog`,
   and a closed issue never stays there. Render bugs no longer wait for a
   renderer rewrite: every bug goes to `4.x` or `Triage`.
   Whoever files an issue sets its milestone; one filed without a milestone
   lands in `Triage` through `.github/workflows/issue-milestone.yml`, and an issue
   closed while still in `Triage` or `Backlog` takes the milestone of the merged pull request that
   closed it; when there is none to copy (closed as not planned or a duplicate),
   it stays in `Triage` with `needs-triage` until a person picks the milestone it
   would have belonged to (never `Backlog`).
   A pull request gets a milestone too, through `.github/workflows/pr-milestone.yml`:
   the milestone of the issue it closes (not `Triage`, `Backlog` or a closed milestone), or the
   current release milestone when it closes none (change `CURRENT_RELEASE` there when a release
   ships; the job fails rather than use a closed milestone).
   When a row finishes, close the issue (completed or not planned) and set the
   `GH` cell to `—`; when an issue is closed on GitHub first, true up the row
   the same way in the next session that touches this file.
   `src/tests/backlogIssueMirror.test.ts` enforces the row half of the
   invariant: well-formed rows, a known status vocabulary, no unresolved row
   without an issue, no finished row holding a live reference, and no two rows
   sharing an issue number.
4. `Type` legend: `review-deferral` (a real finding parked for scope),
   `perf` (a measured/suspected optimization), `feature-request` (unbuilt
   capability awaiting a spec), `bug` (a player-reported defect awaiting a
   fix), `design-decision` (a ruling recorded for the record, often parked
   with its trigger), `task` (a scoped follow-up with a known method).
   `Status`: `open`, `in-progress`, `idea` (feature not yet specced),
   `done` (delivered in full), plus the table's established refinements:
   `partial` (a named remainder is still open), `parked` (deliberately
   shelved with a resurrection condition), `next` (queued), `shipped-v1`
   (first version shipped; the row tracks refinements), `resolved`
   (investigated or fixed and verified; kept for the record, unlike `done`
   it usually closes a defect or a question rather than delivering a
   feature), and `impl-review` (implemented, awaiting review/validation).
5. `Priority` is the do-first order (impact × effort × risk, and whether the
   item is blocked), distinct from `Severity`, which is impact alone. **P1**:
   work next; real correctness/data-safety impact and ready to pick up. **P2**:
   worthwhile, do opportunistically when you next touch the area. **P3**:
   low/cosmetic, blocked on investigation, watch-only, or a feature awaiting a
   spec. The `Priority` value stays strictly `P1`/`P2`/`P3` so the column is
   machine-sortable. Within P3, _ready_ cheap fixes are ordered before _gated_
   ones (blocked on profiling / a spec / watch-only), and each P3 row's Notes
   lead with **Ready.** or **Gated.** so nobody burns a session grabbing a
   blocked "easy P3."
6. Priorities validated by an agent party (game architect + game designer +
   persistence architect, 2026-07-06): P1/P2 and the P3 tier confirmed
   unanimously; the design voice dissented on the VIP-limo row (argued P2 on
   player-visibility), resolved by ranking it first among the P3 cosmetics
   rather than a tier bump.
7. **The engine and components rows moved (2026-11-20).** When each
   Verticopolis repository took its own BMAD setup, the rows and notes about
   the engine (rules, the binding, the conformance suite, parity) went to
   Verticopolis/engine's `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/backlog.md`,
   and those about the renderer, sprites, audio and the shared windows went
   to Verticopolis/components' backlog, each with this file's git history.
   That includes every row citing a `Verticopolis/engine#` or
   `Verticopolis/components#` issue. Work that spans the game and a package
   stays here when the game's side is open; file the package's side in that
   repository's backlog.

| 2026-11-11 | install-page-other-build | — | pwa | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **Done (2026-11-14, #1949): the build puts `index.html` after every `assets/` file in the precache manifest (`pageLastTransform`, held by `scripts/verify-precache.ts`), and the installing worker refuses a page that names an `assets/` file not yet in the precache (`src/public/sw-navigation-fallback.js`). Tests: `src/swNavigationFallback.test.ts`, and `e2e/offlineUpdate.spec.ts` serves B's page to A's installing worker. A rollback that lands mid-install is not caught: the precache holds the older build's files until the worker activates.** **Deferred from the review of #1948 (PR-Agent failure mode).** While a worker installs, `src/public/sw-navigation-fallback.js` fetches the HTML page without its integrity, so a host that rewrites the page cannot fail every install. A deploy that lands between the worker script and the page fetch can then leave the next build's `index.html` beside this build's assets; online it still loads, an offline cold start before the next install would miss its scripts. Fix: after the install fetch, fail the install when the page names an `assets/` URL that is not in this worker's manifest. Test: serve B's page to A's installing worker in `e2e/deployments.ts`, start offline, expect the install refused. |

Unit tests in `src/swNavigationFallback.test.ts` and the e2e rollback test in `e2e/offlineUpdate.spec.ts`.** Deferred from the review of PR #2201 (#1949). The installing worker checks the page against the files already in Workbox's precache, which still holds the previous build's files until the new worker activates, so a page older than the worker (a rollback landing mid-install) passes and an offline cold start after activation asks for deleted scripts. Fix: check the page against this worker's own precache manifest. Test: serve an older build's page to an installing worker and expect the install to be refused. |

| 2026-10-20 | dialog-precedence-auto-surfaced | — | gameplay-feel | bug | P1 | med | — | done | **DONE (closes #1121 on merge): the emergency choice and the TOWER congratulations wait while the open dialog holds the player's work.** `runFrame` asks `dialogHoldsWork(app)` (`src/game/interactionState.ts`, the precedence's `ownsPendingWork` for the dialog on screen). Was: **Ready.** Found by the 3.0 readiness audit, companion to `tower-congrats-over-dialog`. The emergency choice and the TOWER congratulations (`runFrame`, `src/game/frameLoop.ts`) open through `openModalTemplate`, which replaces whatever is in `#modal`, without asking the modal precedence (`src/ui/modalPrecedence.ts`) whether the open dialog holds pending work. A fire or bomb threat that rolls while the player edits an elevator schedule drops the edits with no Discard prompt. A second sequence, found in the independent review of the #1120 fix: a `.TDT` import or export report waits on the precedence leash while an emergency choice is up and the answer releases it; on the next pass the congratulations open as displaceable and replace it, and the report's `onDisplaced` shows "The import was dropped: another window took over" inside the congratulations (Later on the update prompt does the same). Fix direction: wait while `ui.precedence.ownsPendingWork(isDialogOpen())` holds (an unanswered emergency declines itself at the next day pass), or open displacing with a notice the way the reports do. Needs a frame-loop test with a dirty schedule dialog and a pending choice, and the same with `evaluatedTower`. Permanent frontend code. |

| 2026-10-13 | gameplay-event-hub | #964 | engine-events | task | P2 | med | — | open | **Ready (audio audit and planning review, 2026-10-13; plan `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture/architecture-audio-events-2026-10-13.md`, stage 1).** `Simulation.drainGameplayEvents()` is destructive and the analytics bridge is its only caller, so a second consumer (audio) would steal the batch. Fix: a host-level hub drains once per frame, on page hide and on a consent close, and hands every consumer the same read-only batch `{ events, ownFrom, dropped, reason, backdrop }`. `ownFrom` (where the live tower's own events start) lives in `GameplayEventBuffer`: `inherit` sets it, the WASM host carries it through attach `carried` and detach owed events, eviction lowers it (never below 0), and any number of swaps between drains leaves earlier towers before it. `dropped` is the per-batch delta of the cumulative counter, with the baseline kept across a swap (`inherit` and detach carry the count) and rebased only if the counter reads lower. `reason` is `frame`, `hide` or `consentClose`; the bridge keeps its consent and backdrop discards, and audio ignores hide batches. The hub takes hide and consent drains through the analytics session's flush hook (no listeners of its own) and keeps the bridge's drain guard (a throw or a non-array is counted, nothing published) and isolates a throwing consumer. No audible or analytics change. Lands after #950 (same files) and builds on #907's typed drain and buffer restore instead of duplicating them; before or with #954. Adds no work to `perf.spec.ts`, `baseline.json` or `perf:compare` (#972, #979), neither of which waits for it; keeps calling the engine's `drainGameplayEvents` export once per drain occasion under that name, since #979's harness times it by name. `/gds-code-review` and `/bmad-code-review`. |

(1) `download-artifact` v8 extracts a single pattern match FLAT (it adds `\|\| artifacts.length === 1` to its flat-path condition; v4.3.0 did not). `pr-drift-check.yml` assembles shards from `/tmp/dl/shots-$shard/` near lines 194, 361 and 471; with four shards per leg the pattern always matches several, so the layout holds. Guard: `d=/tmp/dl/shots-$shard; [ -f "$d/.shard-complete" ] \|\| d=/tmp/dl`, or make `scripts/screenshot-shards.ts verify` refuse a single-shard run. (2) `setup-node` v5+ auto-enables npm caching from `packageManager`, so a missing `cache:` input no longer means no caching. `package.json` has neither `packageManager` nor `devEngines` today; if either lands, set `package-manager-cache: false` on the pinned-container determinism jobs in `screenshot-capture.yml` and `test.yml` in the same change. (3) `checkout` v6 moved the token from `.git/config` to a file under `RUNNER_TEMP` included via `includeIf.gitdir`; comments near `update-perf-baseline.yml:64`, `update-visual-baselines.yml:84` and `pr-drift-check.yml:456` still name the old location. Harmless today (those checkouts set `persist-credentials: false`, and the pushes build their own `x-access-token` remote from a fresh `git init` dir), but a token-exposure audit grepping `.git/config` would look in the wrong place. **Item 3 done 2026-10-13:** all three comments now name the `RUNNER_TEMP` credentials file and `includeIf.gitdir`, confirmed against a live run log on `main` (`engine-rs` run 219, `59d6540`), whose checkout cleanup prints the `includeif.gitdir:` entries and the `/home/runner/work/_temp/git-credentials-*.config` path. | 2026-09-15 | throttle-persistence-across-reload | — | analytics-posthog-migration | design-decision | P3 | low | — | resolved | **#844 closed 2026-11-15 (4.x triage): decision recorded, throttles stay per page life.** **Deferred from the Codex review on PR #840, verified before deferring.** Both throttles live in module memory, so a reload gives each a fresh budget, while the analytics session id lives in `sessionStorage` and deliberately survives a same-tab reload (`analyticsRelay.ts`). The caps are therefore per PAGE LIFE, not per session, and one `distinct_id` can carry more than ten of either. #840 renamed both constants to `MAX_CRASHES_PER_PAGE_LIFE` / `MAX_ERRORS_PER_PAGE_LIFE`, corrected the docs that claimed "one session sends", and pinned the seam with a test, so nothing is mis-stated; what remains is the design call. Reachable via the crash screen's reload button (`saveLoad.ts` `location.reload()`, resumed through `RESUME_AFTER_RECOVERY_KEY`). Leaving it is defensible: the flood the cap exists to stop needs NO reload (the screen re-shows in place, which is how one page life reached 8,269 events), reloads are human-paced so they cannot threaten the per-IP ingest budget, a crash surviving a reload is a genuinely new incident worth seeing, and `boot` already fires per page life so the structure is recoverable. Persisting it is also defensible: the cap would then mean what a reader assumes. Codex's alternative was a server-side cap, which trades a stateless ingest route for robustness against the client's memory model. If persisted, namespace the stored state by session id so a new tab starts clean, and note that the crash path is exactly where `sessionStorage` is least trustworthy (`saveLoad.ts` already guards it). Sibling #842 closed on PR #840: its consent-epoch premise was wrong for the `$exception` path (that one latches after its gate), and the `crash` path's real consent defect was fixed there rather than deferred. Neither outcome touches this reload question. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-09-14 | exception-throttle-consent-epoch | — | analytics-posthog-migration | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | resolved | **RESOLVED 2026-09-15 on PR #840: the premise was WRONG and there is no hazard to decide. Issue #842 closes on merge.** The row claimed an error raised before a desktop player answers the first-run notice spends a throttle slot that a later grant cannot hand back. It does not. `report()` in `analyticsErrors.ts` returns at `if (!telemetryHostAllowed()) return;` BEFORE it reads `throttle.exhausted` or calls `throttle.allow`, and `desktopAnalyticsAllowed` is false for both `pending` and `declined`, so no un-sendable `$exception` ever spends a slot. Verified by probe: twelve pending-window reports spend nothing, and the same twelve after a grant all transmit rather than deduping away. The `$exception` path also never enters the held queue (`holdWhilePending` has exactly one caller, `trackEvent` in `analyticsCore.ts`), so there is no queue for a grant to flush and nothing for an epoch reset to correct. The asymmetry the row called undesigned is the correct design: this path latches AFTER its gate. The `crash` path latched BEFORE `trackEvent`, which is where its gate and the hold live, and that was a real defect (Codex P2, same PR): a first-run grant flushed the held crash and then re-opened the throttle, so an identical recurrence sent a duplicate. Fixed on that side instead, by releasing the crash slots only when the consent answer means the held crashes are about to be DROPPED (`releaseCrashThrottle` in the new `src/analyticsCrash.ts`, wired in `analytics.ts`), with three regression cases in `analytics.consentEpoch.test.ts`. ORIGINAL DEFER (kept for the record): **Deferred from the `/bmad-code-review` confirming pass on PR #840 (Blind Hunter layer), verified before deferring.** PR #840 moved the cap-and-dedup guard into `src/analyticsThrottle.ts` so the `crash` and `$exception` paths share it, each with its own instance. Only one is wired to the consent epoch: `GameplaySession.startEpoch` calls `this.crashes.reset()`, while `analyticsErrors.ts` resets only from its test hook. So an error thrown before a desktop player answers the first-run notice records its fingerprint and spends a slot, and after a grant that same error can never report, because the fingerprint is still in `seen`. That is the latch-set-before-the-send hazard `GameplaySession.end()` already documents, in the one path that did not get the treatment. PRE-EXISTING: the inline guard behaved identically before the extraction, which only made the asymmetry visible. Not fixed inline because it is a consent-semantics decision with a real argument each way: reset it for symmetry with the gameplay session, or leave it because the error reporter is deliberately not epoch-aware and re-opening on every flip lets a player who toggles the switch re-send the same error each time, the flood the cap exists to stop. If reset, the hook belongs with the other `onDesktopConsentChange` wiring in `src/analytics.ts`, not inside `analyticsErrors.ts`. The helper's doc records the asymmetry as tracked, not designed; update it with whichever answer is chosen. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-09-14 | crash-loop-magnitude | — | analytics-posthog-migration | review-deferral | P2 | med | — | done | **Done (owner approved the `crashes` field on #841, 2026-11-03; closes #841 on merge).** A `session_crashes` event counts the crash screens a page life showed before the `crash` cap and dedup, and is sent once at the terminal page hide when at least one showed. The `$exception` mirror is not part of the count: it stays capped at 10 raw counts and unclassified (status doc row 13, #1090). Was: **Deferred from the `/bmad-code-review` on PR #840 (Blind Hunter layer), verified before deferring.** PR #840 had to cap and dedup `crash` per session: one session emitted 8,269 of them on 2026-09-12 and spent the ingest route's per-IP minute budget, so that session's `session_builds`, `tool_session_uses`, `session_fps` and `session_end` were 429'd away. The cost is that occurrence COUNT is now unrecoverable: a two-loss blip and an 8,269-loss catastrophe both emit the first loss and the first repeat, and `repeat` is a boolean so it cannot say which. Magnitude is what separates a device that hiccuped from one that is unusable, which is the #538 signal. Not fixed inline because it adds a number to the vocabulary, wider than the defect #840 was fixing, and the shape is a real choice: a `crashes` count on a per-session summary at session end (preferred, matches `session_emergencies`, which already accepts that a killed session reports nothing), a `suppressed` count riding the last crash the cap lets through (but that is not the last occurrence, so it is a lower bound taken at the wrong moment), or widening `SessionThrottle` to expose its suppressed tally (cheapest mechanism, still needs one of the first two to carry it). The `$exception` mirror has had the same limitation since it shipped, so a fix should cover both paths or say why not. `src/analyticsSession.ts` `noteCrash`, `src/analyticsThrottle.ts`. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-08-09 | session-end-clock-origins | — | desktop-analytics | design-decision | P2 | low | — | superseded | **RETIRED 2026-10-14 (#991): the Electron and Capacitor wrapper builds are retired, so the code this row tracked is gone.** Kept for the record; issue #808 closes as not planned when this change merges. **Deferred from Codex on PR #802 (`src/telemetry.ts:88`), verified before deferring.** `session_end` has three different clock origins and the largest gap is deliberate, so aligning one without the others makes the metric worse. (1) Web starts at boot with nothing awaited before `create()`. (2) Desktop returning + consented starts at boot plus 0 to 3 seconds of save-store hydration, because `startGameplaySession()` sits at `src/bootstrap.ts:153`, ahead of `await prepareSaveStore()` at `:171` and `create()` at `:173`, and the clock has no first-activity gate (`resumedAt` set in `GameplaySession.begin()`, moved to `src/analyticsSession.ts` by PR #840). (3) Desktop first run does not start at boot at all: `startGameplaySession` early-returns while consent is `pending` (still `src/analytics.ts`, which PR #840 reduced to the module's wiring half) and `armSessionOnGrant` (`src/ui/uiDesktopAnalytics.ts:71-72`) arms it when the player dismisses the notice, an offset of seconds to minutes that dwarfs case (2). NOT a regression from #802: `src/bootstrap.ts` is not in that diff, the ordering matches `main`, and the gate only made desktop analytics reachable. Codex overstated the magnitude: per-call 3s waits (`BOOT_STORE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `src/game/desktopSaveHydrate.ts:31`) do not compound as implied, since a dead bridge exits early at `desktopSaveStore.ts:103` for 3s flat and a timed-out read aborts hydration at `desktopSaveHydrate.ts:208`; a healthy bridge costs 5 to 50 ms, below the metric's own 1-second rounding. Do NOT take the suggested one-line fix of starting the desktop clock after hydration: it would define desktop as time-after-ready while web stays time-from-boot, two meanings under one name, and it would erase the signal for a player who hits a 3s stall and quits at 4s. Preferred: either record boot/hydration duration as its own property so the pre-play window is subtractable on both editions, or document and align what `session_end` means across all three cases. Settle it once real desktop sessions arrive, since case (3) dominates and cannot be evaluated without first-run data. |

| 2026-08-07 | ingest-body-cap-chunked-bypass | — | analytics | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **DONE (v2.33.0, closes #789 on merge).** `handleIngest` reads the body through `readCappedText`, which counts bytes as they arrive and cancels the stream past `MAX_BODY_BYTES` with a 413, so a chunked request without `content-length` is bounded by the route itself. Original notes: **Ready.** Deferred from the stage-2 review of the desktop ingest route (#781, Edge Case Hunter, low, pre-existing). `handleIngest` reads `content-length` and returns 413 only when the parsed number exceeds `MAX_BODY_BYTES`. A request that omits the header (a chunked `Transfer-Encoding` POST) gives `Number(null) === 0`, which is finite and under the cap, so the check passes and `await request.json()` buffers the body with no ceiling of its own. Vercel's platform body limit is the outer backstop, so production is bounded by that rather than by this check. Recorded because the stage-2 module comment newly leaned on the cap as one of the two things that bound abuse; that wording was corrected in the same round to say the cap reads `content-length`, but the gap itself is unfixed on `POST /api/ingest` (the only route since #991). Fix shape: bound the read itself (stream and count bytes, or take `arrayBuffer()` and reject past `MAX_BODY_BYTES` before parsing) instead of trusting a declared length. |

Original notes: **Ready.** Deferred from the `/bmad-code-review` of the ingest follow-ups (#1009; Blind Hunter and Edge Case Hunter, low, pre-existing). `readCappedText` bounds the bytes `POST /api/ingest` reads but not how long it waits: a body under `MAX_BODY_BYTES` sent one small chunk at a time with no `content-length` holds the function until the platform timeout. The old `request.json()` read had the same exposure. Fix shape: race each `reader.read()` against a deadline from the start of the read, cancel the reader and answer with an error status when it passes, with a fake-timer test for a stalled stream. |

| 2026-08-07 | desktop-ingest-no-cors | — | desktop-analytics | design-decision | P2 | — | — | superseded | **RETIRED 2026-10-14 (#991): the Electron and Capacitor wrapper builds are retired, so the code this row tracked is gone.** Kept for the record; issue #791 closes as not planned when this change merges. **Open question for the client-transport stage; deferred from the stage-2 review of the desktop ingest route (#781, Edge Case Hunter, informational).** `handleIngestRoute` answers any non-POST with 405, and no handler sets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, so a cross-origin request that would need a preflight fails before it reaches the route and no caller can read the response. The client stage therefore has to post in a form that stays a simple request: `navigator.sendBeacon`, or a `no-cors` `fetch` with a simple content type. The core parses body text and ignores `content-type`, which is what makes both of those work, and the relay's answers carry no body, so nothing is lost by being unable to read them. Also noted in `api/ingest/desktop.ts` so the transport author meets it before writing the client. Decide there whether to keep the endpoint write-only (cheapest, and it is all the relay needs) or to add a preflight branch and an allow-origin echo. Severity is `—` because this records a constraint rather than a defect. |

| 2026-07-29 | desktop-menu-undo-scope | — | desktop-shell | design-decision | P3 | low | — | superseded | **RETIRED 2026-10-14 (#991): the Electron and Capacitor wrapper builds are retired, so the code this row tracked is gone.** Kept for the record; issue #722 closes as not planned when this change merges. **Ready.** Deferred from the /gds-code-review of PR #715 (rounds 3 and 4). `hostCommands.ts` routes `undo`/`redo` straight to `cb.onUndo`/`onRedo` with no equivalent of the `ownsNativeUndo` yield `inputKeys.ts:56` implements, so the menu Edit > Undo with the tower-rename field focused rolls back a TOWER action rather than the text edit. Decide one: (a) menu undo stays tower-scoped and the no-accelerator rationale stops citing the rename field, since the accelerator-collision argument holds without it; or (b) the field needs protecting from any source, and `dispatch` grows the `ownsNativeUndo` check. Also in scope: `undo`/`redo` currently run during a live editor press because the editor-busy guard only refuses `OPENS_A_DIALOG`, and an undo can mutate the facility the press is acting on, which is arguably worse than the stats refresh the frame loop already declines. Needs a product decision, not a fix. `/gds-code-review`. |

On a system with no color-emoji font the supplementary-plane glyphs tofu while BMP symbols (★ ✕ ☰ ⚙ ↻) still draw, so the toolbar looks half-broken with NO error: nothing 404s, the console is clean, and the smoke passes. Confirmed during Linux verification 2026-07-29 (`fc-list`: 20 fonts, zero emoji), and specifically ruled out as an asset problem (case audit: 58 files, 1036 refs, 0 casing bugs; zero console errors). Mainstream desktop distributions ship an emoji font, so most desktop Linux users are fine; the exposure is bare or minimal distros, containers, and WSL, and a PACKAGED app cannot assume a host font the way a browser tab effectively can. Fix shape: inline SVG for the functional icons rather than bundling Noto Color Emoji (~10MB for a handful of glyphs), which also fixes the cross-platform inconsistency where the same emoji looks materially different on Windows, macOS, and Android. Decorative emoji in copy can stay as text. Player-facing (version bump + screenshot regeneration in the pinned container). Not caused by the command seam; #715 only made it visible by putting the game in a packaged app on a bare system. `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | help-dialog-double-scrollbar | — | accessibility | bug | P2 | low | — | done | **RESOLVED (this PR; #717 closes on merge).** The outer scrollbar was the `#modal` dialog element itself: current Chromium's UA stylesheet gives an open `<dialog>` `overflow: auto` (verified with a bare dialog in Chromium 149: computed `overflow-y: auto` with zero page CSS), so once Help's content passed `.modal-box`'s 82vh cap the dialog element became a second live scroller next to the box's own, and it also clipped the box's `.win` drop shadow. No repo CSS added it, which is why it reproduced in both the shell and the browser with no code change. Fix: `#modal` now pins `overflow: visible`, so the box stays the dialog's single scroller; this covers Statistics, Saves, and Settings too, since every dialog shares the `#modal` > `.modal-box` shell (measured: none of them adds a scroller of its own). New guard `src/tests/modalSingleScroller.guard.test.ts` pins the rule from the CSS source: `#modal` declares `overflow: visible`, nothing may re-scroll it, the dialog subtree's base cascade scrolls exactly one element (`.modal-box`), and media blocks may re-scroll only the ratified `.es-grid` well (#513). Mutation-checked red on the pre-fix CSS and on an injected second scroller. Original: Owner-reported while driving the packaged desktop shell 2026-07-29: the Help dialog shows TWO vertical scrollbars, an outer one on the dialog and an inner one on the guide body. This is a regression of the same class as #513 (the schedule dialog's double scrollbar, fixed in `040b53da`, deferral closed in `d6c44928`), so the earlier fix did not generalize to the Help dialog and nothing guards the pattern. Reproduces in the browser as well as the shell, so it is not desktop-specific and the desktop seam did not cause it. Fix shape: one scroll container per dialog. Find which of the dialog shell and the guide body owns the overflow, give the other `overflow: visible` / `min-height: 0`, and add a regression test asserting exactly one scrollable descendant, since a per-dialog manual fix is what let this come back. Check the other long dialogs (Statistics, Saves, Settings) in the same pass. Player-facing (version bump + screenshot regeneration in the pinned Playwright container). `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | desktop-help-page-link | — | desktop-shell | bug | P2 | low | — | superseded | **RETIRED 2026-10-14 (#991): the Electron and Capacitor wrapper builds are retired, so the code this row tracked is gone.** Kept for the record; issue #720 closes as not planned when this change merges. **Ready.** The Help dialog's "Open the full help page" link is silently dead in the Electron shell: no window, no navigation, no console error, and nothing in the shell's refusal record. Verified against the packaged exe 2026-07-29 with a real trusted click. Two causes stacked: it is `target="_blank"` in a shell whose `setWindowOpenHandler` denies every open (the correct posture), and it is not passed through `routeExternalInWrapper` (that is applied only to `.help-report a` and the crash screen report link); and `/help` is an extensionless route, so even with a window it would resolve to `dist/help` and 404, because the clean URL only works where a host rewrites it. A dist scan found exactly three such references: `/help`, `/gallery`, and `/` (the last already resolves, since the protocol handler maps an empty path to `index.html`). Secondary finding worth keeping: the click was blocked WITHOUT producing a `windows` refusal entry, so the smoke's "zero blocked window opens" assertion is weaker than it reads. Proper fix is #718 (Help and Gallery in a real secondary window), which must add an extensionless-route rule to the resolver rather than two special cases. Cheap interim if #718 is not next: withhold the link in a wrapped build the way the install button already is, since a dead control is worse than no control and the dialog carries the same content. Not caused by the command seam (#715). `/bmad-code-review`. |
| 2026-07-29 | help-gallery-secondary-window | — | desktop-shell | feature-request | P3 | low | — | superseded | **RETIRED 2026-10-14 (#991): the Electron and Capacitor wrapper builds are retired, so the code this row tracked is gone.** Kept for the record; issue #718 closes as not planned when this change merges. **Needs a spec.** Owner proposal 2026-07-29: open Help (and add Gallery) as a real secondary window in the desktop shell, so reading either does not cover the tower, plus make "Back to game" close that window rather than navigate. Findings that shape it: (a) `/help` and `/gallery` already exist as standalone prerendered pages (`dist/help.html`, `dist/gallery.html`), so no new content is needed, and Gallery has NO in-game dialog at all, only the page, so a Gallery menu item cannot route through the command seam the way the other nine commands do. (b) Navigating the MAIN window to either page would destroy the running sim, and the shell's `will-navigate` guard would permit it because both are same-origin, so a second window is the only safe shape. (c) `pageShell` renders "Back to game" as `<a href="/">`, which in a popup would boot a SECOND game instance inside the popup rather than returning to the first, so the page shell needs to learn it is in a secondary window and close instead. (d) PREREQUISITE: the shell's `will-navigate` and `setWindowOpenHandler` guards are attached to `mainWindow.webContents` only, so a second window would inherit none of them. A global `app.on("web-contents-created")` guard (plus `will-frame-navigate`, `will-redirect`, and explicit `webviewTag: false`) has to land first, and is worth doing regardless of this feature. (e) Keep the in-game Help dialog as the default on every platform: a second OS window is worse on a handheld and with a controller, and removing the dialog would fork the interface shared with web, Android, and iOS. So this is additive, desktop-only, and opt-in. Belongs to the private distribution repo's shell work plus a small public change for the page-shell close behavior. `/gds-code-review` for the public half. |

| 2026-07-29 | longpress-peek-dismiss-latch | — | mobile-touch-play | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | resolved | **FIXED in story 696-1 (v2.6.1); issue #696 closes on merge.** Party ruling: a long-press is explicit fresh intent like a tap, so `onLongPress` spends the ✕-dismissal latch through the same `resetLatch()` seam `selectPicked` uses (one intent gate, two callers); hover picks alone still respect the latch and pure touch is unaffected. Pinned by a wiring test and a controller-level seam test. ORIGINAL DEFER (kept for the record): **Deferred from the `/bmad-code-review` of the long-press-peek re-land (v2.6.0, PR #636 follow-up; Acceptance Auditor and Edge Case Hunter independently).** On a hybrid touch+mouse device, the hover card's coarse-tier ✕ sets the inspector's `inspectDismissed` latch (`src/game/inspector.ts`); a touch long-press on that same facility routes through `inspectPicked`, which early-returns on the latch, so the peek raises no card (GDD AC1 promises one) while `longPressFired` still swallows the release: the hold is silently eaten. Pure-touch devices unaffected (the peek card has no ✕ and `hide()` never arms the latch). Fix shape: decide whether a long-press is an explicit re-request like a tap (then spend the latch, mirroring `selectPicked` -> `resetLatch()`) or a glance like a hover (then at least skip the swallow when `inspectPicked` declines to show); the first reads more honest. Touch input path only, no engine or save impact. `/bmad-code-review`. |

Saving over it destroys bytes that a later build may well have recovered, with no confirmation, which is exactly what `SaveGame.preserveUnreadable` exists to prevent for the autosave. The title screen's new picker labels that same slot "Couldn't be read by this version." and offers no control, so the two surfaces now describe the same slot differently. The fix is available: `SlotInfo.present` (added in that PR) distinguishes absent from unreadable, so the manager can label the row and either drop Save or route it through a confirm. Not fixed in the Load Tower PR because it is the in-game manager's behavior, untouched by that change. `/bmad-code-review`. |

| 2026-07-22 | analytics-posthog-s5-fps-report | — | analytics-posthog-migration | perf | P3 | low | — | done | **DONE (backlog sync, #2081): #603 closed done 2026-11-18 by PR #2455.** **Deferred from the S5 gds-code-review (session_fps, #538).** `GameplaySession.noteFrame` (src/analyticsSession.ts) samples every foreground rendered frame, including idle frames where the sim is not advancing: game speed 0 (paused) and while a blocking modal freezes time (`runFrame` calls it before the freeze early return, and speed 0 still fires rAF). On a large tower the per-frame sim tick is the dominant cost, so idle frames run faster than active play and pull p50/low toward the vsync ceiling, diluting the busy-simulation frames the metric exists to characterize, worst on exactly the big-tower sessions #538 targets. Not fixed inline because the clean fix couples the sampler to frame-loop state (game speed / modal-open) and needs frame-anchor resets on every active<->idle transition. Options when picked up: gate `noteFrame` on `SPEEDS[app.speed] > 0` (reset the anchor when sampling pauses), or keep all-frame sampling and document the metric as idle-inclusive. The larger correctness bug in the same area (the engine's 200ms spike-clamp inverting the worst-frame `low`) was fixed in the S5 PR; this is the remaining measurement-validity refinement. `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-07-16 | deserialize-coercion | — | save-hardening | review-deferral | P2 | med | — | done | **IMPLEMENTED (issue #306); issue closes on merge.** `Simulation.deserialize` assigned the top-level `money` and clock `minutes` straight from the parsed save, skipping the finite/range coercion every other field gets, so a forged or corrupt save (`money: NaN`, `minutes: NaN`) poisoned the ledger, rating gates, and every `clock.minutes` consumer (completeAt/vacate timers, the lunch bulletin) with NaN. Now money is coerced to a finite number (falling back to the constructor starting balance, sign preserved so legal debt survives) and minutes to a non-negative finite number before the `Clock` is built. Minutes is deliberately NOT floored: real play accumulates sub-minute steps, so a valid fractional save round-trips exactly (idempotence preserved, golden master unchanged). The `star` field was already hardened by a prior change; this completes the set. `/gds-code-review` ran (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor); the one patch fixed a fractional-minutes idempotence regression the first cut introduced (dropped the `Math.floor`) and strengthened the tests to pin the fallback values; two magnitude-cap defers recorded in the inbox. Guarded by `storage.integration.test.ts` (forged NaN/Infinity/negative/string money, star, and minutes, plus a fractional round-trip and legal-debt round-trip). |

| 2026-07-15 | elevator-dispatch-balancing | — | gameplay-feel | review-deferral | P2 | med | — | resolved | **FIXED 2026-07-16 (v1.51.2, branch `claude/dispatch-shaft-fairness`, closes #303).** `route`/`staffRoute` now spread each ride leg across its bank of equivalent same-kind shafts (those stopping at both leg endpoints) with a seeded `crowd.rng` draw, so identical trips stop funneling onto one shaft while siblings idle. A leg with no sibling draws nothing (zero-draw gate). Reachability probes now use a pure `crowd.reachable` (no rng), so `floorReachable`'s ~6 Hz editor repaint pump can never perturb the seeded crowd stream on a banked tower. GOLDEN DISPOSITION: both Classic and Modern hashes UNCHANGED, not re-pinned. The shared golden fixture builds a single elevator (no bank), so every leg is a bank of one and the zero-draw gate skips the rng call; the multi-shaft balancing is covered by `src/tests/integration/elevatorDispatchBalancing.integration.test.ts` (spread across 2- and 3-shaft banks, run-to-run determinism, single-shaft zero-draw, probe purity, and a mutation check that raw `bfsRoute` funnels). gds-code-review ran all three adversarial layers: 1 patch (probe purity) fixed, 5 dismissed, 0 deferred. ORIGINAL DEFER (kept for the record): **Recovered defer, recorded 2026-07-15 (it had lived only in session memory since the crowd/vehicle read-right review round, PR #285 era; the owner approved it then as its own PR).** `bfsRoute` picks deterministically among equivalent shafts, so identical trips funnel onto ONE shaft of a bank: that landing queue piles up while sibling shafts sit idle, and the drawn crowd makes the imbalance obvious. Balancing route choice across equivalent shafts changes sim behavior AND the rng stream (golden-master churn), so it ships alone in a quiet golden-master window. SEQUENCING LAW (people-tracking priority party 2026-07-15, unanimous after rebuttal): the elevator queue-view reconciliation (the `pixelart-elevator-queue-seam` defer in the inbox below) lands FIRST, so the queue panel trusts exactly one population before dispatch changes move `carLoad`; otherwise a dispatch regression and a display artifact are indistinguishable. Full ruling: "People-tracking priority ruling" section at the end of this file. SEQUENCING UPDATE 2026-07-15: the queue-view reconciliation precondition (E6-S7, GH #314) is DELIVERED on branch `claude/queue-view-reconcile` (`boarded` now reads the drawn `crowd.carRiders`), so this row is unblocked to ship in a quiet golden-master window; it stays open until the balancing fix itself lands. |

FIXED THIS SESSION (separate, real harness bugs): `run.sh` now forwards the `SCREEN`/`MAXIMIZE`/`ZOOM_CLICKS`/`CLICK_SECS`/`SHOT_DELAY`/`SHOT_OUT` env hooks and no longer mounts the host X socket into the headless modes (that mount made the container Xvfb fail to bind, so shots fell back to a blank 1024x768 frame); the image must be rebuilt after `entrypoint.sh` edits (README noted). Fix in `engine-rs/src/presence.rs` and `engine-rs/src/wasm_port_render.rs`. **RESCOPED P3 to P2 per full-repository-audit 2026-07-21 (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/reviews/full-repository-audit-2026-07-21/`), finding AUD-014.** The original 'measure first' gate is satisfied, and the trigger is the RENDER FRAME, not just the sim: `TowerEngine.tick()` calls `sim.congestion()` every frame (~60 Hz, `src/render/excalibur/TowerEngine.ts:363`), and v2 `spatialCongestionByFloor` + `functionalParkingSet` are un-memoized full-tower scans (25k-unit rescan + flood fill per frame); dominant per-frame CPU cost, primary driver of the measured 11 to 5 to 2 FPS scaling on real saves. **Fix (the one current direction, with the 2026-10-27 correction below for the hourly pass): memoize both on the (revision, mealOverlayRevision, outer-step token) key set per the `Crowd.queueView` precedent.** Acceptance: memo computes at most once per (step, revision) with a call-count pin, values byte-identical to the un-memoized build (golden master), before/after frame cost on the owner's sixseven_15 save. Provenance: the 2026-07-06 deferral warned that a memo keyed only on (revision, rush) would go stale because `isPresent` occupancy drifts within a rush bucket, and suggested caching the scalar hourly instead; the step-token key answers that caveat (presence and occupancy only change during sim steps, so the memo never spans a mutation), which supersedes the scalar-cache idea. Issue #338 rewritten to match. **Also (2026-10-27, Hot-Path Auditor F4, `/gds-code-review` round 2 of `story-hud-stats-rooms.md`):** the HUD's traffic chip builds the same map on every HUD refresh (`updateTraffic` → `peakCongestionHotspot` → `buildSpatialCongestion`, about 6 Hz; cloud 0.573 ms per call on the large tower and 0.885 ms on the owner's save, the largest HUD cost now that `stats()` reads kept counts), so the memo serves both readers. **Update 2026-10-27 (facade slice 4e-1, #1211, Hot-Path Auditor):** the parking seed reads a per-revision ramp index (`rampUnits`) and the shaft loops read the cached stop lists (`tower.stopsOf`), both with identical results; one build (the render's `congestion()` read or the chip's answer) now costs about 0.13 to 0.14 ms on the large tower, from 0.42 to 0.44, and 0.62 to 0.69 ms on `split-tower-4`, from 0.85 to 1.01 (cloud, exploratory, two runs). The per-frame read named above has since moved: it is `beginFrame` in `src/render/excalibur/towerFrame.ts`, which reads `sim.congestion()` once per rendered frame. The owner accepted leaving the chip's share here, per UI tick through the port, on 2026-10-27 (PR #1329's section 7 quote). Still for this memo, per frame and per traffic chip refresh: the build's own room walk, the flood's tuple and string key per step (about 2,500 steps per build on `split-tower-4`; `Tower.roomAt` builds its own position string per tile as well, about 0.12 ms of a 0.41 ms flood there, which a numeric flood key alone leaves), the same build repeated within one frame (the render read, the chip, the mobile editor's diagnostics), and the per-build allocations, including an attribution map that `congestion()` and the hotspot drop. Two more callers: the desktop hover card builds the map up to twice per pointer move (`dominantGripe`'s `congestionAt` and the gripe text's `bindingTransportClassAt`), and the hourly sweep builds it once per sold condo vacated for congestion (`congestionChurnNote`, `src/engine/sim/churn.ts`), about 3,400 visits each on the large tower. The second is a caution for this memo's key: `vacate` reads the map before the unit empties, then empties it, all inside one hourly pass, so a memo keyed on the step token would hand the next note in that pass a stale map and could change the log wording the save carries. The claim above that the memo never spans a mutation does not hold inside the hourly pass, so the acceptance pins a mid-pass `vacate`. The shaft list per floor with each shaft's capacity depends only on what the revision keys (transports, stops, cars, the served set) and can be built once per revision without that risk. When the memo lands, fix the line in `src/render/renderSim.ts` that calls `congestion` "memoized by the engine": until then it rebuilds every frame. `/gds-code-review`. |

| 2026-10-09 | analytics-engine-events | — | analytics | feature-request | P2 | medium | — | done | **#873 closed done 2026-11-15 (4.x triage): engine gameplay events shipped (#903, #938, #1015, #1706); the TypeScript step went with #1956.** **In progress (owner request, 2026-10-09).** The Rust engine becomes the single source of truth for game-level analytics events: on a meaningful gameplay action it emits a provider-agnostic structured event (`game_started`, `building_placed`, `population_milestone`, `game_saved`, and so on) with names, semantics and payload schemas defined once beside the engine; platform layers forward them through thin adapters (the web TypeScript layer to the web provider, native and store builds to theirs); platform-specific analytics (page views, UI interactions, browser errors, store behavior, mobile attribution) stay on their platform; the engine knows no vendor; providers change without touching gameplay logic; no event may enter the save or the hashed views; tests assert the events gameplay actions produce. Inspect `src/analytics*.ts` first, name the conflicts, propose the migration, preserve behavior. Scheduled after the WASM switch lands. Party-mode design session held 2026-10-09: findings and the migration order in `_bmad-output/party-mode/analytics-engine-events-party-findings-2026-10-09.md` (engine drain buffer outside the save and views, a catalog file as the contract, platform folds over the stream, an `events` hash in the conformance lock while both engines run, `milestone_reached` in the catalog). **Phase 1 (migration step 1) done** (#903): story `story-analytics-engine-events.md` (catalog, both engines' emission and drain buffer, the lock's `events` hash, the WASM host's single drain). **Phase 2 (migration step 2) done** (#938): story `story-analytics-engine-bridge.md`; `src/analyticsEngineBridge.ts` folds the engine's events into today's wire names and the shell's detection retired for `new_game_started`, the build folds, `star_reached`, `session_emergencies`, `emergency_choice`, `price_tune` and `capacity_tune`, with the value changes documented in `analyticsCore.ts`; `demolish` now comes from the bridge too (story `story-analytics-removal-fold.md`, #1015): `removeFacility` emits `facility_removed` with its method (story `engine-owned-charges`, #928), the bridge folds it into `economy_action: demolish` with the method as `detail` under its own 40-a-minute limit (2.31.2), and the shell's two `demolish` calls are gone. **Step 3 done (2026-11-03, 2.78.10; the owner approved the new wire event the same day):** the bridge forwards the engine's `milestone_reached` as the wire event `milestone_reached` with its catalog `id`, one row per achievement under the bridge's per-name rate limit; the data map lists it. Steps 4 and 5 stay open on this row (step 5 waits for the TypeScript engine to be deleted). A swap keeps its events (fixed in phase 1 after the party review): every undo, load, import and new game goes through `adoptSim`, which hands the replaced tower's undrained events and its drop count to the adopted tower (`GameplayEventBuffer.inherit`); under the WASM host, `startWasmHost` lets the old tower go before the swap, so a trapped engine cannot block a load or a new game, and an estimate of its loss is counted. The boot tower `main.ts` founds when there is no save is only the title screen's backdrop, so `runBootFlow` marks its buffer and the tower New Tower founds inherits none of it. Gates the step 2 bridge must meet (party review of phase 1, 2026-10-10): egress runs `checkEvent` on every drained event and fails closed, dropping and counting any event outside the catalog; the bridge forwards only the platform folds, under a rate limit; the catalog is frozen for the release that ships the bridge, before the 3.0.0 default flip, so the flip is measured against a fixed contract; the folds count actions, so a build the player then undid still counts as a build (its `facility_placed` stays in the stream, with no inverse event); a drained batch can span two towers (a swap hands the replaced tower's events on ahead of the new tower's founding), so per-tower folds take `tower_founded` as the boundary only for a new game. When the bridge edits the catalog, the `tower_founded` entry gains the backdrop rule, so a second platform reading only the catalog leaves a title backdrop's founding out too. The bridge also skips a buffer marked `discardOnHandOff` (the title backdrop) when it drains, as the swap does. Follow-ups from the same review: `simulation-capacity-commands` (#906) and `gameplay-events-glue` (#907), both done. |

| 2026-07-30 | store-record-that-will-not-import | — | desktop-shell | review-deferral | P2 | med | — | superseded | **RETIRED 2026-10-14 (#991): the Electron and Capacitor wrapper builds are retired, so the code this row tracked is gone.** Kept for the record; issue #727 closes as not planned when this change merges. **Raised by the final gds-code-review confirming pass on `story/desktop-save-store` (story D2, public half), verified with probes across repeated boots.** `migrateSavesToStore` verifies every write with a read-back and, on mismatch, reports `write-failed` and LEAVES the record in place. Leaving it is deliberate: deleting was tried and reverted, because `wrote` proves only that our own write resolved and not that what sits there now is ours (so a writer that committed in between had its record deleted by us), and because clearing the destination made the next boot find it empty and repeat the identical failure, looping across six consecutive probed boots, where leaving it lets boot two report `already-present` and reach a stable fixpoint after one. CONSEQUENCE for the SECOND PR: boot two reports `already-present` and `alreadyComplete: true` while the good tower sits unmigrated in localStorage. Harmless while nothing consumes the store and localStorage stays canonical; NOT harmless in D2's AC4 to AC6, where the store becomes authoritative. That read path must handle a record that is present but will not import rather than assuming presence implies loadability. `SaveGame.listSlots` already has the posture (`exists` means LOADABLE, distinct from `present`) and the saves UI already has the wording ("Couldn't be read by this version."). Options: render it as `present && !exists` keeping Delete, fall back to the localStorage copy and retry, or report once to the player. |
| 2026-07-31 | store-read-path-preconditions | — | desktop-shell | review-deferral | P2 | med | — | superseded | **RETIRED 2026-10-14 (#991): the Electron and Capacitor wrapper builds are retired, so the code this row tracked is gone.** Kept for the record; issue #736 closes as not planned when this change merges. **Two findings from the third confirming pass on PR #734, both probe-verified, both unreachable while `storeIsAuthoritative()` is false (which #734 pins with a test) and both real the moment the read path is routed.** (F1) A localStorage fallback strands the newer copy: `persistAutosave` falls back when `localFallbackSafe` is true, correct for a shared-scope tower since it is already readable by every account on the machine, but the store may already hold an OLDER copy. Tick 1 writes to the store, tick 2 fails and goes to localStorage, boot 2 reports `already-present` (correctly, per the derived done-marker) and the newer copy is never moved. On a persistently failing store an entire session lives in localStorage and is unreachable once reads are routed. This is the "two copies to reconcile, and reconciliation is how an older copy resurrects over a newer one" hazard `autosavePersist.ts` names. Options: prefer the newer copy (needs a game-owned monotonic stamp in the payload, since `savedAt` is optional by contract and `seq` is session-scoped, so a save-format change), retry the localStorage copy into the store before the done-marker check, or refuse the fallback when the store already holds that address. Same family as [[migrated-unreadable-duplicate]] and #727. (F5) The tower origin outlives the tower: `loadedFrom` is set on first successful write and cleared only by the test seam, so a New Game inherits the previous tower's origin and lands in the wrong namespace. The read-path PR introduces the first production callers of `noteTowerOrigin`, so it must also clear it on New Game and on adopting an imported tower. |

Fix: report a step as an edit (the revision moves, as for a build) so the per-frame sync follows it, keep only the parts of the follow a step needs, fold the two walks into one, and measure an undo on the Tier-1 win tower before and after. **Progress (#1790):** the renderer half is done. | 2026-11-10 | space-release-unseen-press-firefox | — | ui | review-deferral | P3 | low | — | done | **DONE (closes #1841): no change needed. Current Firefox source (`mozilla-firefox/firefox` main, 2026-11-19: `nsGenericHTMLElement::HandleKeyboardActivation`) sets an active-for-keyboard flag on a Space keydown the focused control receives and presses it on the Space keyup only while that flag is set; checkboxes and radios take the same path from `HTMLInputElement`. A control that never saw the keydown is not pressed, so Firefox behaves as Chromium does. Checked by reading the source, since this session has no Firefox. A comment by `focusTookSpace` in `src/game/inputKeys.ts` records it.** **Ready (deferred from the independent review of #1839).** A window blur, or the page going hidden (`visibilitychange`), ends the Space hold and clears its press record (`endSpaceHold`, `src/game/inputKeys.ts`). A Space still held when focus comes back sends repeats, and the first one makes a new record that withholds them and cancels the release; a release with no repeat in between reaches a newly focused button with nothing to cancel it. Chromium does not click a button on a release it never saw go down. Firefox reportedly clicked in older versions; current Firefox has not been checked. Check current Firefox with a real keyboard; if it clicks, cancel a release with no press on record on a control that acts on a Space release, with a test, after checking that no virtual keyboard sends an unmatched Space keyup. |

Space has this covered by the press record and `focusTookSpace` (#1839), and other keys under a newly raised cover by `heldUncovered`. Fix: record the focus at Enter's press and withhold its repeats from a control focused since, with tests for the dialog case and for the control that got the press keeping its repeats. |

## Deferral inbox

### Deferred from: `bmad-code-review` of keeping the scene through an undo or redo step (PR #1790, 2026-11-04)

- **[TRIAGED 2026-11-04 -> absorbed into #1170 (`ts-render-ui-hotpath-sweep`), item T9, comment 6621978912.]** Hot path, deferred by reference: one structural scene sync walks `tower.units` about nine times (`syncMotion` three times, `buildWalkers` twice, the scenery apron, `syncStructureTiles` and the next frame's `refreshFloorLiveliness`, beside the T7 walks of comment 6533902822), about 390,000 visits per edit, undo or redo on the large tower. Pre-existing: every build and bulldoze already pays it, and #1790 only stops an undo or redo from adding a full scene rebuild on top.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of facade slice 5a (#1212, PR #1294, 2026-10-27)

- **[TRIAGED 2026-10-27 -> absorbed into #1224 (facade slice 12), comment 6434689581.]** `saveNow` and `undoSignature` read the instance without waiting on a step gate, so on the experimental worker host (#1068, on hold) an undo copy or signature can be older than the commands issued before it. Pre-existing: the undo capture never checked the gate. The port now scopes both members to main-thread adapters, and the in-order primitive a worker needs is recorded on #1224 (slice 12) with the cost per caller (story D2).
- **[TRIAGED 2026-10-27 -> absorbed into #1158 (`engine-rs-hotpath-audit`), items A31 and A32.]** Hot path, deferred by reference: a hosted save is still parsed and stringified again for stored writes (#1158 A31, its stored half; 5b's `stampText` takes it once EX-2 is decided), and every undo, redo or load re-serializes the tower it just loaded to build its engine (#1158 A32).
- **[TRIAGED 2026-10-27 -> absorbed into #1158 (`engine-rs-hotpath-audit`), item A34.]** Hot path, new item: the first sync after every attach forces a full merge, about 429 ms on the large tower per undo or redo (cloud median); filed as #1158 A34, with the `engine-rs-hotpath-audit` row's notes updated.

### Deferred from: `bmad-code-review` of the window.game tooling gate (2026-07-29)

- **[TRIAGED 2026-11-15 -> row `e2e-tooling-build-fail-fast`, #2281.]** The e2e specs and the perf harness still fail as a mute Playwright `waitForFunction` timeout when run against a dist built without `VC_TOOLING=1` (a stale local `dist/`, or a Copilot sandbox whose pre-baked tooling dist was overwritten by the plain quality-gate `npm run build`). `scripts/screenshots.ts` names the fix in its error; e2e has no equivalent fail-fast diagnostic. Add one central check (a shared boot helper or Playwright global setup) that detects a booted page with no `window.game` and names the flag.

### Deferred from: `bmad-code-review` of the boot-overlay hardening (2026-07-22)

- **[TRIAGED 2026-11-15 -> row `boot-fatal-splash-keys`, #2282.]** **Boot-error path: splash controls stay armed behind the fatal overlay (Edge Case Hunter, minor, pre-existing).** When a boot error lands after `runBootFlow` mounts the splash, the fatal overlay now stacks above it (fixed, z-index 100) without removing it, so the splash controller's document keydown listener stays armed: a returning player pressing Escape behind the fatal screen runs the normal Continue dismissal invisibly (teardown, music program flip, welcome toast, and the splash-presence autosave gate opens for the half-booted sim). Pre-existing exposure: the old flow-content message left the splash and listener alive the same way; the overlay only makes it invisible rather than new. Fix shape if picked up: a boot-fatal latch the splash controller and the autosave interval both consult (or an inert/input-capture layer on the overlay that spares its own Reload button).

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of the save-integrity bundle (#537, 2026-07-21)

- **[RETIRED 2026-11-15: the TypeScript engine and its `deserializeGuards.ts` were deleted (#1875), so this code no longer exists.]** **Id re-mint drift near ID_CAP (Edge Case Hunter, minor, pre-existing).** `repairEntityIds` (`src/engine/sim/deserializeGuards.ts`, extracted verbatim from `serialization.ts`) mints replacement ids with `++maxLoadedId` and does not itself re-check ID_CAP, so a forged save carrying one sane id just under 2^31 plus corrupt/duplicate ids can mint repaired ids at or past ID_CAP; the next load then classifies those as insane and re-mints again, so ids are unstable across loads (unique within a session, never corrupting). Pathological forged-save corner only; a legit tower sits orders of magnitude below the cap. Fix shape if picked up: when `maxLoadedId` is within `entities.length` of ID_CAP, renumber the whole entity list from 1.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of Classic vs Modern in-game reachability (PR 1, 2026-07-20)

- **Patched, not deferred (Blind Hunter, minor):** the shared `compareTemplate` closer said "report it below" / "suggest it there too", which dangled in the Compare modal and the founding `<details>` (their only element below is a Got it / Found button, no report affordance). Made the closer context-neutral ("report it so we can fix it" / "suggest it too"); it still reads correctly in the Help dialog, where the report link sits below. `help.test.ts` does not assert that sentence, so it stayed green.
- **Reverted, not deferred (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter, the double-scrollbar fix):** the owner-requested desktop dialog double-scrollbar fix was attempted as `#modal { max-height: 92vh; overflow: hidden }`. Both hunters showed it is net-negative: every `.modal-box` is already capped at 82vh < the 92vh dialog, so the dialog never scrolled (no double bar to remove there), while `overflow: hidden` clips the `.win` drop-shadow (`--win-shadow: 2px 2px`) on the right of every dialog. Reverted. The real double-scroll is most likely the elevator Schedule dialog's nested `.es-grid` (`max-height: 200px; overflow-y: auto` inside the scrolling `.modal-box`), a dialog-specific box-plus-inner-grid pair. Needs a live repro to confirm which dialog and fix the nested scroller precisely; tracked to a separate verified change, not this PR. **RESOLVED v1.70.1 (issue #513, closed by its fix PR):** the live repro confirmed the suspicion exactly, and only it: `.es-grid` is the sole vertical scroller nesting inside `.modal-box`, and at desktop heights the box and grid scrolled together (measured, e.g. box 765/656 + grid 268/200 at 1280x800). Fix: fine pointers drop the grid cap (the box is the dialog's single scroller; the column header pins flush under the sticky title, measured gap 0), while the phone and coarse-pointer blocks keep the 200px well for swipe reachability and to avoid a box-pinned head colliding with the coarse-only sticky day toggle (a 19px overlap caught in review at emulated 1024x768 coarse). `#modal` untouched, drop shadow intact.
- **[RETIRED 2026-11-15: fixed since; `src/game/inputKeys.ts` gates undo and redo on any open `#modal`.]** **Undo/redo stays live behind the paused Compare modal (Edge Case Hunter, minor, pre-existing).** `inputKeys.ts` runs the Ctrl/Cmd+Z handlers before its modal-open bail, so Ctrl+Z behind the (paused) Compare modal still undoes a build, mutating the tower the player believes is frozen for reading. Speed restore is unaffected (`adoptSim` does not touch `this.speed`). This is a pre-existing gate gap (undo works behind Help/Settings too); the pause only makes it more surprising. Fix shape if it matters: move the modal-open bail ahead of undo/redo, but that changes behavior for every modal, so it is out of scope for this PR.
- **[RETIRED 2026-11-15: latent only, as recorded; reopen with a repro if a caller can open a dialog over it.]** **Second modal opening over the Compare modal would orphan the speed restore (Edge Case Hunter, minor, latent/unreachable).** If any modal replaced the shared `#modal` while Compare is open, `finishModal` would reset `onclick`/`oncancel` and discard the `finish` closure, leaving the tower at speed 0. Confirmed unreachable today: the update auto-pop gates on `!isModalOpen`, sim-driven modals cannot fire while paused, and the badge is inert behind `showModal()`. Latent only; revisit if a future caller can open a modal over Compare (a restore-on-any-close mechanism independent of the modal's own handlers would be the durable fix).
- **[RETIRED 2026-11-15: masked, as recorded; the title screen covers the badge until the first status pump.]** **First-paint Modern badge flash (Edge Case Hunter, minor, masked).** `index.html` ships the badge as "This tower: Classic" with no `data-mode`; a Modern boot shows Classic until the first status pump corrects it. Masked in practice (the Tower panel sits behind the splash until founding, and the pump repaints before it is visible). Fix shape if it ever shows: seed `data-mode` from the founded/loaded mode at boot.
- **[RETIRED 2026-11-15: accepted deviations, nothing to do.]** **CAP-2 ships two deliberate deviations from the spec's literal wording (Acceptance Auditor, non-blocking).** (1) The badge writer is a `setMode(ui, mode)` function in `uiStatus.ts`, not a `UI.setMode(mode)` method: `UI.ts` was at 492/500 lines against the file-size guard, and the badge is a per-frame status element, so it lives with the status pump. (2) Its label/class/dirty-gate tests live in `uiDialogs.integration.test.ts` (the only real-UI DOM fixture) rather than `uiStatus.test.ts` (which uses a fake UI). Behavior and coverage are unchanged; both are accepted, not fixed.

### Deferred from: `bmad-code-review` of Classic vs Modern in-game reachability (PR 1, 2026-07-20)

- **Patched, not deferred (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter):** `showCompare` now opens the modal BEFORE pausing (`onSpeed(0)`), so a render/open failure can never strand the tower at speed 0 with no dismiss path; a `getSpeed` delegation assertion was added to `uiCallbacks.test.ts` (a missing-call typo in `createUICallbacks` would otherwise pass); and the `index.html` `#btn-mode` seed gained a comment warning not to hardcode `data-mode` (which would skip the first Modern-boot repaint, since the dirty-gate keys on it).
- **[RETIRED 2026-11-15: informational; the file-size guard already enforces it.]** **`UI.ts` sits at exactly 500/500 lines against the strict `>` file-size guard (Edge Case Hunter, informational).** This is why `setMode` was extracted to `uiStatus.ts`. The next single line added to `UI.ts` (even a comment) trips `fileSize.guard.test.ts`, an unrelated CI gate. Heads-up for later PRs: any change that must touch `UI.ts` should budget a small extraction to restore headroom (or split the class), rather than be surprised by the guard. Not fixed here (splitting `UI.ts` is out of this PR's scope). The Blind Hunter's onclick/oncancel "leak" and the Auditor's two CAP-2 wording deviations were verified safe / already recorded in the gds block above; the first-paint flash and second-modal-over-Compare items are likewise already deferred there.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of analytics adapter seam (S1, spec-analytics-posthog-migration, 2026-07-22)

- **[TRIAGED 2026-11-15 -> row `telemetry-provider-neutral-name`, #2284.]** **Rename `injectVercelTelemetry` to a provider-neutral name when the transport actually swaps (Blind Hunter, low).** The function is now a thin caller into the vendor-neutral `analyticsAdapter().injectPageTelemetry()`, but its exported name still says "Vercel" and its callers (`bootstrap.ts`, `gallery.ts`, `helpPage.ts`, plus the three tests) reference it by that name. Accurate for S1 (Vercel is still the only adapter), so renaming now would be premature churn against call sites the seam deliberately left untouched. Fold the rename into S3/S6 when the PostHog transport lands and the name would otherwise mislead. Fix shape: rename to `injectPageTelemetry` (or `injectTelemetry`) across the three page entries and their tests in the same PR that swaps the adapter.
- **[RETIRED 2026-11-15: its trigger, retiring Vercel, is off; Vercel Web Analytics stays on.]** **Retire the direct `@vercel/*` mocks in the telemetry tests when Vercel is retired at S6 (Acceptance Auditor, low, informational).** `src/analytics.test.ts`, `src/telemetry.test.ts`, and `src/bootstrap.test.ts` still import and assert on the vendor functions (`track`, `inject`, `injectSpeedInsights`) directly, which is correct for S1 (Vercel remains the live transport and the default `vercelAdapter` still forwards to them). This is why CAP-1's "switching transports touches exactly one file" holds for production code and the new adapter test but not for the whole suite: those three tests re-point to the new transport when Vercel is dropped. Expected S6 test churn, recorded here so it is not a surprise. The S1 mocks were hardened this PR to stub the full vendor surface (`track` + `inject` + speed-insights) so a partial mock cannot silently drop a sibling-path event.

## Triaged (2026-07-21, #545)

(see "How items flow"). The pass processed every `### Deferred from:` section
dated 2026-07-19 or older, wherever it sits in this file (most follow directly
below; a few live in the lower clusters): each was folded into a curated row,
marked absorbed or superseded with a pointer, or retired with a reason, and its
leading blockquote records the disposition with the evidence kept beneath it.
Four `### Deferred from:` sections dated 2026-07-20 are the current in-flight
window and are left un-triaged (three sit just above this heading; the Classic
vs Modern pages defer sits in a lower cluster). Other note kinds in this file
(`### Followups from:`, decision logs, initiative rows) are outside this pass's
`### Deferred from:` scope and are not dispositioned here. Counts are tallied in
the triage summary at the end of this section.

### Deferred from: `gds-code-review` of housekeeping legibility layer (housekeeping-overhaul epic 3, PR #462) (adversarial, 2026-07-17)

> **ABSORBED + RETIRED (#545).** The missing maid return-leg (maids despawn inside the cleaned room) folds into `housekeeping-modern-refinements` (#547). The escalation-morning double-signal is by design and honest per-line (a room truly went unserved AND is now infested); retired.

- **Maids never visibly leave a cleaned room: no return leg (Acceptance Auditor, low; player-noticeable polish).** After the cleaning dwell expires, a staff person hits the non-round-tripper branch in `crowd/motion.ts` and despawns inside the room; the GDD's "walk in, dwell, and leave a room clean" (and the agent model's `returning` state) is under-delivered by the vanish. Spawn is now pinned to the unit footprint (epic 3), so the remaining gap is only the exit. Fix shape: reuse the meal round-trip machinery (a `returning` staff leg back to the crew floor that despawns at the unit), taking care the job RESULT still fires at dwell end (cleaning must not wait for the walk home) and the maid ledger frees at result time, not despawn time, or throughput regresses.
- **Escalation-morning double-signal (Edge Case Hunter, nit; by design, recorded).** `leftover` is latched before `escalateInfestations()`, so a room on its third dirty day counts in "N unserved yesterday" AND flips into the `Infested` row the same morning; the two lines can name the same rooms and read as more problems than exist. Honest per-line (it truly went unserved AND is now infested); revisit the aggregate copy only if playtest shows players double-counting.

### Deferred from: bmad-code-review of screenshot-gallery-mode-fork (#443) (adversarial, 2026-07-16)

> **ABSORBED + RETIRED (#545).** The Classic batch-rung dialog opening with its select displaying "No Rate" (a pre-existing PR #440 first-frame bug) folds into `batch-pricing-v1.1` (#383). Fix belongs in the dialog (sync once unconditionally BEFORE focus lands, or skip only DIRTY focused selects), in `src/ui/uiBatchPricing.ts` + `src/ui/templates/rungPicker.ts`, with the gallery pair re-minted after.
